The Best Window Insulation Kits of 2026, Tested and Reviewed

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

100+ Most Unique and Practical Gifts 🎁 for Homeowners and DIYers

Published Nov 8, 2026 9:00 AM EST

We may earn revenue from the products available on this page and participate in affiliate programs. Learn More ›

Window insulation kits offer an affordable way to cut the cost of your energy bill and make your home less drafty. For those who can’t afford to replace their windows, these kits are a great alternative.

Most window insulation kits work the same way, by attaching a sheet of heat-shrink plastic or other insulating material to the window panes with double-sided tape or magnetic strips. But since they don’t all perform equally well, we put several of the most popular options to the test in our own homes. Based on those tests, we chose the 3M Indoor Window Insulator Kit as the best overall option for its relatively easy installation and the fact that a single kit covers multiple windows.

Read on for shopping considerations when selecting this type of window treatment and to learn why these are among the best window insulation kits available.

Bottom line: Window insulation kits are an affordable way to reduce energy costs and drafts, with the 3M Indoor Kit leading the pack for its ease and coverage.

Windows that have been treated with insulation cannot usually be opened, meaning these kits are a seasonal solution to drafty windows. The following recommendations are among the best options available in their respective categories.

This 3M insulator kit comes with enough film to cover up to five windows measuring 5 feet long by 3 feet wide. This relatively thick film provides enhanced insulation thanks to its thickness of 0.75 mils (a unit of measurement equal to a thousandth of an inch). The film comes in one cut-to-fit sheet that measures 17.5 feet long by 5.16 feet wide. This kit also comes with a 27.7-yard roll of double-sided tape to facilitate installation and ensure it stays in place all season.

This was my favorite kit to install. I thought I would prefer the rolls of plastic sheeting, and it is true that this one was tricky to lay out and cut to size, but nonetheless, this kit allowed me to reposition the plastic a few times before pressing it down and heating it. Also, the kit does cover a lot of square footage, but the ends of the sheet can be tough, so it’s important to take your time. After I finished installing the kit, removing wrinkles was extremely easy. This kit provided the best results alongside the other 3M products that use the same plastic.

Read our full review: 3M Indoor Window Insulator Kit

What our tester says: “This was my favorite kit because I could reposition the film before pressing it into place, even though laying out and cutting the large sheet took some patience. Once it was up, the wrinkles came out easily with heat, and the finished results were among the best.”—Tom Scalisi, Product Reviews tester and writer

Get the 3M indoor window insulation kit at Amazon.

Cozy as the winter months can be, the cold outside can be brutal.

To keep a home insulated and warm in the winter, consider the Duck Max Strength window kit. It comes with everything required for insulating 5-foot-long by 3-foot-wide windows in bedrooms, offices, kitchens, and more, including film, tape, and an instruction manual. But it can be cut to fit smaller windows if necessary.

Made with puncture-resistant shrink film, this 126-inch-long by 62-inch-wide indoor option is perfect for high-traffic homes with children and pets. It’s easy to install and remove cleanly, making it suitable for first timers. For added reliability, this kit can be used on painted wood, aluminum, vinyl, and finished/varnished wood, but it can’t be used on plaster, drywall, or unfinished wood.

The Duck Brand window kit’s film is considerably thicker than other competing options, so I installed it over the window my 80-pound lab-pointer mix likes to jump at. To be fair, no film stood a chance against my dog, but it did stick to the windowsill that he has effectively destroyed over the past year. I was able to easily repair any damage with packing tape. Also, the Duck kits come in rolls, which are easy to cut to width and then drop down over the window for easy unfolding. It was really difficult to get the wrinkles out (it took over 30 minutes of heating with a blow dryer to get it reasonably clear), but it was easy to remove once I could get under the tape and pull it back.

Get the Duck Brand Max Strength window insulation kit at Amazon.

Those who want to insulate on a budget can trust this shrink film kit from Duck Brand. With its crystal-clear shrink film and included double-sided insulating tape, this option can reduce energy consumption. At 210 inches long by 62 inches wide, this large film can be cut if required but works best for windows that measure 5 feet long by 3 feet wide. For convenience, this option is also offered in various size options.

This pick can also be applied to painted wood, aluminum, vinyl, and finished wood for more insulation, but it isn’t suitable for plaster, drywall, or unfinished wood varieties.

The kit was fairly easy to install, and it seemed reasonably durable. The roll-out installation made it manageable to unravel, and it featured quality adhesive tape that stuck well and was easy to remove the backing from. While it’s similar in thickness to most of the films we tested, it felt smoother out of the box. It also had a more substantial, higher-quality feel. Unfortunately, it was difficult to remove the existing wrinkles, and there were marks from the manufacturing plant in the film that I could not heat out.

Get the Duck Brand shrink film window insulation kit at Amazon.

If shoppers prefer to install insulating film on the outside of windows, they’ll want a long-lasting product that can hold up to the elements. This outdoor-use window film was created by 3M for that very purpose. Capable of covering up to two 5-foot-long by 3-foot-wide windows, 3M’s kit comes with a sheet of 0.75 mil-thick film that’s 7 feet long and 5.16 feet wide. It also includes just under 14 yards of high-quality double-sided tape. This kit installs like an indoor window film kit: cut, tape, shrink, and trim.

The only difference I could discern between this kit and the other 3M insulation kits was that the adhesive felt like it was of a better quality (though there was nothing wrong with the quality of the other kits). Otherwise, it was the same thickness with the same ease of wrinkle removal. The only challenge I found was that it was difficult and frustrating to install a sheet outside if there was any wind at all, so 3M might consider using a roll-type installation for this kit. But in my opinion, there is no reason it can’t be installed on an interior window as well, so it’s a good kit all around.

Get the 3M outdoor window insulation kit at Amazon.

New insulating tape is essential for those who love to reuse their insulation sheets. When new insulation is needed, Yotache’s high-density foam tape is a good option. This thick tape comes in two rolls, providing a combined 13 feet of length. It can be cut with ease using a scissors or blade.

The closed cell foam is made from chloroprene rubber foam and neoprene, making it weather- and dustproof, oil- and corrosion-resistant, and flame-retardant. As for the adhesive quality, this tape has a waterproof grid backing that can endure temperatures ranging from -58 to 302 degrees Fahrenheit. Though these are meant for windows, they can also be attached to furniture, cabinets, cars, boats, sports equipment, HVAC systems, plumbing, and more. Plus, there are a lot of thicknesses and lengths to choose from based on the user’s needs.

I installed the Yotache weather stripping at the bottom of one of my windows to see how well it worked. My windows are new and not leaky, so the tolerances were a little tight (though I was able to lock the sashes). However, if I did have leaky windows, I wouldn’t hesitate to buy this foam. It’s incredibly tough thanks to the fiberglass adhesive mesh on the back, yet it’s easy to cut and install. It also removes cleanly but won’t fall off on its own. Plus, there’s a benefit to being able to open the window if necessary. I found the foam too dense for my application, but I can see it making a world of difference for older windows with too much clearance.

Get the Yotache window insulation tape at Amazon.

Comfort aside, one of the most important reasons to insulate drafty windows is to save energy and lower monthly heating costs. Saving energy also benefits the environment. With Frost King’s window film, the company claims users can reduce their carbon footprint related to heating and cooling by up to 35 percent. This kit comes with enough individual sheets of plastic to cover up to nine windows measuring 5 feet long by 3 feet wide. It also includes 165 feet of double-sided tape.

This kit is very similar to other kits on the list, but that’s okay because they’re all good quality. I found that this one installed just like the others, yet with slightly smaller sheets that were a little easier to manage. The film is the same thickness and quality as most of the other kits, and considering it can cover up to nine windows, it’s a great deal. The wrinkles were very easy to remove with a blow dryer, but I did find the tape wasn’t as high quality as the kits from other brands.

Get the Frost King window insulation kit at Amazon.

The StangH Portable Thermal Insulated Blackout Curtains didn’t meet my expectations. First, the way the suction cups are affixed to the curtain with keyrings is extremely frustrating and time-consuming. Also, unless the glass is warm, sticking a suction cup to a cold surface is very difficult. This curtain also looks terrible from the outside, as the suction cups are extremely visible. A standard thermal curtain will likely work almost as well, look much nicer, and be easier to install.

I wanted to ensure that any window insulation kit we recommended was truly effective and easy to install, so I put each of these kits to the test. I installed each kit according to the manufacturer’s instructions to see how easy they were to install and to determine the components’ quality. I also tested how well the plastic shrank and how easy the wrinkles were to remove. I left them on the windows for 2 days to see how well they worked (I even left a few on permanently to get through the winter). I then removed them to determine what that process was like and how much cleanup there would be.

It’s worth noting that without a laboratory full of expensive machinery and repeatable, consistent conditions, it’s hard to objectively determine which window insulation kits insulated better. However, all the kits we included on this list were effective at stopping any noticeable air movement or condensation.

There are a number of factors to consider when shopping for window insulation kits. They’re available in different foil types, such as plastic, magnetic, and reflective. Features like film thickness, dimensions, reusability, and installation requirements will determine which insulation kit is most suitable for your home.

Several window insulation product types are on the market: plastic film, magnetic barriers, and reflective foil insulation.

Windows come in all sizes, so look for kits with enough film to cover your windows. Most kits come with enough plastic sheets and double-sided tape to seal several windows. It’s essential for shoppers to know their windows’ dimensions to determine which kit will work for their home.

Window insulation kits create a trap for air between the window and the film. That trap is called an air lock. Double-sided tape, which holds the film in place on the window pane, will create an air lock. By pressing the film firmly onto the tape, users can create a strong bond that keeps drafts out.

To create a long-lasting seal, durable film is a must. Durability is often determined by the film’s thickness. Thick film offers more resistance to punctures from kids or pets. It’s also easier to apply correctly, thus establishing a good seal. Thick film does a better job of insulating windows. Standard window film is about 19 micrometers (μm) thick, while heavy-duty film can be as thick as 38 μm.

Some homes have only one or two truly drafty windows, while others have a slew of them. Choose a kit that has enough film to cover all the drafty windows. Many kits come in multipacks that can cover up to 10 windows. They come with sheets of film that can be cut to size, so every window that needs insulation can be covered.

Window insulation kits are meant to be temporary solutions to drafty windows. The good news is that most of these kits are reusable, so buyers won’t have to purchase a new kit every year. Films 19 μm or thicker can be peeled off the double-sided tape, folded up, and stored when winter is over. The double-sided tape won’t be reusable, but a roll of double-sided tape is cheaper than a houseful of window kits.

The best window insulation kits are effective, affordable, and easy to install. Most kits are installed by placing double-sided tape around the perimeter of the window jamb. Then, window film is applied to the tape. At the bottom of the window, the film is attached to a piece of tape on the sill, making a seal. Finally, the film is heated with a handheld blow dryer to create a transparent, airtight seal free from wrinkles.

Still have questions about choosing the best window insulation kits? Here’s some more information to help you decide.

Window insulation kits create an effective barrier preventing cold air from entering a home. Heat-shrink film applied to the window pane with double-sided tape creates an air gap that works like a thermal window.

Insulated curtains do a good job of keeping the cold temperatures at bay, but they don’t create airtight seals to hold back drafts like window insulation kits do. Plus, insulated curtains must be closed to work, so they block natural light. Window insulation film is usually clear, so it doesn’t block those precious rays of winter sun from shining into a home.

Some window insulation kits claim to reduce a home’s energy consumption by up to 35 percent. Users will need to install these products carefully and on every leaky window to maximize their effectiveness.

Kyle Whittingham Buyout Details in Contract Show Cost for Michigan to Fire Coach

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

MINNEAPOLIS, MINNESOTA - OCTOBER 03: Head coach Kyle Whittingham of the Michigan Wolverines reacts during the second half against the Minnesota Golden Gophers at TCF Bank Stadium on October 03, 2026 in Minneapolis, Minnesota. (Photo by Tyler Clouse/Getty Images)

ANN ARBOR, MICHIGAN - SEPTEMBER 12: Head coach Kyle Whittingham of the Michigan Wolverines talks with the referees in the first half while playing the Oklahoma Sooners at Michigan Stadium on September 12, 2026 in Ann Arbor, Michigan. (Photo by Gregory Shamus/Getty Images)

Kyle Whittingham is in his first year with the Michigan Wolverines as their head football coach.

There will be fans calling for him not to get a second season and instead for Michigan to move on by paying the buyout amount in his contract.

It's been a bigger struggle this season for Michigan than anyone hoped. Whittingham didn't get enough out of Bryce Underwood, so he benched him for Tommy Carr, and it hasn't been smooth sailing for Carr, either.

That was before Michigan went out and didn't score through three quarters against Michigan State on Saturday, Nov. 7.

More and more, the Wolverines' fanbase is getting upset with Whittingham. Where exactly will that lead? Only time will tell — but it could be expensive.

GettyMINNEAPOLIS, MINNESOTA – OCTOBER 03: Head coach Kyle Whittingham of the Michigan Wolverines reacts during the second half against the Minnesota Golden Gophers at TCF Bank Stadium on October 03, 2026 in Minneapolis, Minnesota. (Photo by Tyler Clouse/Getty Images)

Whittingham came to Michigan to begin his coaching tenure with the 2026 season, and he got a hefty contract to do so.

He signed a five-year deal with a base salary of $41 million.

That began with an $8 million salary for 2026, going up by $100,000 each year, according to GBM Wolverine.

GettyANN ARBOR, MICHIGAN – SEPTEMBER 12: Head coach Kyle Whittingham of the Michigan Wolverines talks with the referees in the first half while playing the Oklahoma Sooners at Michigan Stadium on September 12, 2026 in Ann Arbor, Michigan. (Photo by Gregory Shamus/Getty Images)

Whittingham being fired without cause would result in him being paid 75% of his remaining salary, according to GBM Wolverine.

That means, for example, that he'd get paid 75% of $33 million if he was fired after the 2026 season. That works out to a $24.75 million buyout — which certainly isn't an unheard-of total in modern college football.

GBM Wolverine explained the outlook like this in an article earlier this season: "The good news for Michigan is that they aren't paying any other buyouts to former coaches. (Sherrone) Moore was fired for cause, and former head basketball coach Dusty May left for the NBA on his own. So, if Michigan wanted to pay the buyout, they probably have the money to do so."

It still would be a pretty rough look for Michigan to move on from its new coach after just a single season, although the Moore situation didn't set up the Wolverines to have the best head coach hiring cycle ever.

The reality is that Whittingham seemed like a quality hire based on his strong past, but it hasn't worked out the way they hoped in Ann Arbor.

Whittingham isn't necessarily a goner, but if he is at some point, Michigan can probably afford to make that happen.

Michigan fans demand Kyle Whittingham's job after shocking loss to Michigan State appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

Coach Kyle Whittingham's odyssey with the Michigan Wolverines looks in serious jeopardy after a bland, borderline embarrassing rivalry game loss to Pat Fitzgerald's Michigan State Spartans that came after injury concerns dogged MSU's starting quarterback Alessio Milivojevic. Puma: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Puma's U.S. NERLYNX demand is heading toward a second year of growth, yet China royalties are shrinking, a split the company must balance.

On its third-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Nov. 5, 2026, Puma (PBYI -15.56%) reported total revenue of $55.1 million for the quarter. For long-term investors, the business is shifting focus toward clinical drug development while managing international revenue headwinds. Three things management said stand out.

Puma cleared its remaining loan balance to investment firm Athyrium during the second quarter. The payoff frees up operating cash flow and leaves the business with $108.5 million in cash, cash equivalents, and marketable securities to support research and development.

Puma recorded a $2.1 million cumulative reduction to Q3 2026 royalty revenue after generic versions of NERLYNX, its prescription breast cancer drug, gained market share in China. Management lowered full-year royalty guidance to a range of $18 million to $19 million as a lower contractual royalty rate takes effect.

Interim results from the company's ALISCA-Breast1 trial of alisertib, an investigational cancer drug, showed activity in specific biomarker groups. Management plans to engage global health regulators in the first half of 2027 to design a pivotal phase III late-stage clinical study.

Demand for NERLYNX in the U.S. is heading toward a second consecutive year of growth, providing baseline commercial revenue. Management is controlling operating expenses to maintain positive net income for the full year.

With zero debt and $108.5 million in liquidity, Puma is balancing the commercialization of its core product against clinical trial expansion for alisertib.

As COP31 Antalya kicks off, can world move beyond big promises?

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Droughts, floods and extreme heat are upping the pressure on delegates at the UN climate talks in Antalya, Turkey. Can countries move beyond pledges to concrete action?

With its pulsing music, flashy images and barrage of special effects, the key message in Antalya's marketing video is clear: Turkey, the host of the 31st edition of the UN climate talks, is cool, stylish, modern — and eco-conscious.

Both President Recep Tayyip Erdogan and his wife, Emine, make an appearance, with Turkey's first lady calling climate change "one of the most visible manifestations of the burden human activity has placed on our planet."

From November 9-20, delegates from nearly every country in the world are coming together in the Mediterranean city of Antalya to chart a way out of the climate crisis. Turkey is the official host, and Australia will lead the negotiations. But the issues they'll confront during those 12 days are far more serious than the showy marketing campaign make them out to be.

Severe drought, extreme heat, wildfires and record-low water levels in Europe's main waterways — summer across the continent was dominated by climate emergencies.

And in late August, a series of flash floods triggered by a rock-ice avalanche in Nepal and Tibet washed away entire villages, killing more than 1,400 people and leaving thousands missing. Climate experts have linked the disaster to melting ice and a warming climate.

To view this video please enable JavaScript, and consider upgrading to a web browser that supports HTML5 video

Globally, carbon emissions are still rising: the UN has concluded that it is no longer possible to keep global temperature rise to 1.5 degrees Celsius (2.7 Fahrenheit) above pre-industrial levels, the goal agreed at COP21 in 2015.

The global transition away from polluting fossil fuels like coal, oil and gas will be at the center of attention in Antalya — once again. To date, there's been no consensus on whether and how to transition rapidly away from this main source of global warming.

Despite the setbacks, the world has made some progress thanks to the 2015 Paris Agreement.

David Victor, a climate expert at the University of California San Diego, pointed out that before then, the planet was hurtling toward an average temperature rise of between 3-3.5 C.

If current climate commitments are fully implemented, the latest UN projections show a rise of up to 2.6 C. It's a significant step forward, even though warming of that magnitude will still have worldwide consequences.

"There's no question that we could be doing more," said Victor, though he cautioned against pinning too much on this year's summit.

All agreements at COP need to be unanimous, meaning individual states can quash any sweeping resolutions. Added to that challenge are the ongoing wars, trade conflicts and other economic problems swirling around this year's negotiations.

"It's like a giant Christmas tree in which everybody's hung their different ornaments, and the tree is groaning and more ornaments are being put on it," said Victor. "We have to look at it for what it is: It's a place where people talk about stuff."

Instead of waiting for all COP member states to come to an agreement, Victor sees a better chance for change in what he called "clubs" — smaller alliances of governments and companies working together to advance actual solutions, whether it be in aviation, shipping or electromobility.

"The Paris process is important. It has to go forward," he told DW. "But all the work is going to be done in clubs."

To view this video please enable JavaScript, and consider upgrading to a web browser that supports HTML5 video

An example of this approach was last April's first ever International Conference on Transitioning Away from Fossil Fuels in Santa Marta, Colombia.

Colombia and the Netherlands invited representatives from 57 countries and key sectors to discuss ways to move the world away from coal, oil and gas, after an agreement on a similar plan failed at the 2025 climate summit due to opposition from several oil-producing states.

With an eye to the ongoing trade conflicts and energy crisis, hosts Turkey and Australia have included green industrialization and the clean energy transition among their aims. One goal is to increase the share of electricity in global energy consumption to 35% by 2035, up from around 20% today.

To view this video please enable JavaScript, and consider upgrading to a web browser that supports HTML5 video

A recent analysis by the International Energy Agency found accelerating electrification could cut fuel-importing countries' energy bills by more than $400 billion (€357 billion) by 2035, compared to 2025 levels. The idea could appeal to regions dependent on fossil fuel imports — among them Germany and the European Union.

Ahead of COP31, German Environment Minister Carsten Schneider said Germany and the EU wanted to spark new momentum in the "global phaseout of fossil fuels."

It's wouldn't just be good for the climate, but a chance for many countries to boost their economies and reduce dependence on energy imports, added Schneider in a statement.

Oil-producing states like Saudi Arabia, however, aren't interested in deals that will undermine their business model. At recent UN climate conferences, they've repeatedly blocked any final agreements including phaseout plans.

Even China, despite its massive expansion of wind and solar energy over the last 20 years, could be against the idea. The economic superpower traditionally rejects any outside demands, proposals or interference concerning its national decisions on energy policy.

Every country in the world is affected by climate change, with loss and damage calculated in the hundreds of billions from heat, drought and storms. Worst hit are vulnerable countries in the Global South and island nations, who are at risk of disappearing as sea levels rise.

That's where money comes into play, with developing countries calling for more support to protect their citizens from the harms of global warming. At the 2024 UN climate talks in Baku, Azerbaijan, industrialized nations agreed to free up at least $300 billion annually in climate financing by 2035.

But where exactly all those funds will come from remains hotly debated. At the same time, several major developed countries — most notably, the US — have cut their development aid.

The German government only paid out €4.7 billion from its 2025 budget for international climate financing, down from €6.1 billion in 2024.

"The level of financial support for developing countries today is far from what's needed," said Jan Kowalzig, a climate expert at Oxfam Germany.

Breaking financial promises like this comes at the expense of losing the trust of developing countries and, as a result, the diplomatic power, on which Germany and other industrialized nations rely to shape policy to fight climate change.

Cosima Cassel, who focuses on climate diplomacy at the independent think tank E3G in Berlin, believes Turkey's push to turbocharge electrification during an oil crisis could be successful.

To view this video please enable JavaScript, and consider upgrading to a web browser that supports HTML5 video

For Cassel, a successful outcome at what are expected to be difficult negotiations in Antalya would reveal actual political will behind the grand agreements.

When it comes to electrification, that means taking the expansion of electricity grids and battery storage capacity seriously — not to mention removing the barriers to further electrification.

This article was originally written in German.

Personal Finance (Not Investing) • Re: PSA: Schwab apparently launching passkeys?

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Am I not understanding something or is Schwab's passkey implementation flawed?

Using Mac OSX, Safari and a Yubikey (set as my Schwab passkey) I no longer need a username or password to log in to my Schwab account, just my Yubikey/Yubikey PIN.

Shouldn't the Yubikey be the second factor after entering your Login ID and Password?
It seems to me that it is working fine - something you have and something you know (or are in the case of biometrics).

You have the private passkey on the Yubikey. Schwab sends a challenge and you enter your pin to access the passkey. The passkey solves the challenge and sends its signed answer back. Schwab sees it is correct and lets you in.

Functionally, the passkey is the username/password combo. And the pin is like 2FA.

The advantage of the passkey being of course that hackers can’t capture your credentials via a faked Schwab url.

Cyberpunk 2077 Meets Papers, Please in Steam’s Latest Surprise Viral Hit

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Every now and then, a game with little to no fanfare pre-launch gets blessed by the algorithm and surges in popularity. Probably Stolen is the latest recipient of this; after being released on Oct. 28, it's continued to climb in popularity, peaking at 174,382 concurrent players on Nov. 4, according to SteamDB.

The debut game from indie studio Questing Goose, Probably Stolen is what you get if you mix the aesthetics and vibes of Cyberpunk 2077 with the gameplay systems of Papers, Please, with a little inventory management thrown in for good measure. You are the proprietor of a market stall in a dystopian space station, and you have one goal: turn the biggest profit you can.

There's just one caveat: most of your trading is going to come via illicit goods. "You’re a former scavenger who secured a storefront thanks to a generous loan and questionable timing," reads the Steam page. "Now rent is rising, factions are watching, and most of your best margins live in legal gray zones."

You can choose "which kind of trouble you're willing to monetize," from being a gunsmith or chemist to a farmer, water merchant, or fence. Each option brings its own problems though, such as addiction, inspections, extra hurdles to jump through such as machinery and permits. The list goes on. As your ethically dubious operation grows, so does your portfolio, and you can start running multiple businesses simultaneously. But you better make sure you don't miss a rent payment.

Reviews on Steam are currently aggregating to "overwhelmingly positive," and some of the anecdotes from players' time in the game are the best case of exactly why Probably Stolen is so popular. "Hid my storage chest with my most expencive [sic] stuff in the trashcan during a surprise popo-inspection and forgot to take it out. Garbage collector becomes richest man over nigh," reads one review. "I am a humble water dealer," another starts. "Times were tough... so I put opium in the water to increase sales. It worked."

A weekly cheat sheet to what you’ll eventually love to play. No buying, no commitment — just saving for later.

Multiple reviews also praise its "just one more round" effect, and argue that it has raised the bar for the genre. Others say it stands out amongst the deluge of low-effort games Steam has been flooded with recently. There are few complaints, mostly around the late-game, claiming the gameplay doesn't evolve much, but it sounds like players won't reach that for dozens of hours, at which point the $17.99 price will have likely been more than worth it.

Where To Spot The Airbus A380 In 2026: The 10 Airports With The Most Superjumbo Flights

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The Airbus A380 remains one of the most recognizable aircraft in commercial aviation, and despite production having ended, the world's largest passenger airliner continues to operate an impressive international network. For enthusiasts hoping to catch the Superjumbo in action during 2026, some airports offer considerably better opportunities than others.

The Simple Flying Flight Tracker reveals where the aircraft makes the most appearances, which airlines operate the greatest number of services, and where spotters can find the widest variety of individual A380s. This article covers every Airbus A380 movement recorded between October 5 and November 3, 2026, providing a snapshot of the aircraft's continued global presence. Movements count each flight at both its departure and arrival airports, meaning a total of 14,140 movements represent 7,070 individual flights.

Manchester Airport (MAN) recorded 167 flights operated by the Airbus A380 between October 5 and November 3, with Emirates the sole operator of the type. Although its overall activity is considerably lower than that of the major international hubs, Manchester is notable because the Dubai-based carrier provides a regular Superjumbo presence, making it a convenient destination for those in northern England. Its position also gives British spotters an alternative to traveling to London to see the aircraft.

Meanwhile, New York John F. Kennedy International Airport (JFK) saw 284 Airbus A380 flights over the same period. These services were operated by the likes of Korean Air, Emirates, and Asiana Airlines, reflecting continuing demand for high-capacity international services connecting the US with major overseas destinations. This airport's presence in the list highlights how the A380 remains an important part of the transatlantic and transpacific aviation landscape.

Other airports also benefit from Emirates' continued commitment to the Airbus A380. Birmingham (BHX), Prague (PRG), Denpasar (DPS), and Christchurch (CHC) are among the destinations where enthusiasts can encounter the aircraft on daily services, while Glasgow (GLA) receives less frequent visits. These locations demonstrate that the Superjumbo's presence extends well beyond the world's largest connecting hubs, offering spotting opportunities at destinations that might otherwise appear unlikely to feature such a large aircraft.

According to the Simple Flying Flight Tracker, Paris Charles De Gaulle Airport (CDG) recorded 333 flights operated by the Airbus A380 between October 5 and November 3, 2026. Operators of the A380 to the French capital include Emirates, Qatar Airways, and Etihad Airways, demonstrating the continuing importance of Gulf-based airlines in maintaining the aircraft's European presence. Air France formerly operated a fleet of 10 A380s before retiring the aircraft type during the COVID-19 pandemic.

Meanwhile, Los Angeles International Airport (LAX) saw 342 Airbus A380 flights in the same period, making it one of the leading destinations in the United States. Its airline list includes Asiana Airlines, British Airways, Qantas, Korean Air, Emirates, and Lufthansa, giving it six different operators.

The airport's extensive long-haul connections explain its position in the ranking. Airlines deploy the A380 on selected routes linking Los Angeles with major international hubs across Europe, Asia, and the Middle East. Its role as a major gateway to the US ensures that several international carriers continue to bring the aircraft to California from destinations including London Heathrow Airport (LHR), Seoul Incheon International Airport (ICN), and Dubai International Airport (DXB).

Bangkok Suvarnabhumi Airport (BKK) recorded 356 Airbus A380 flights, operated by Asiana Airlines, Etihad Airways, Emirates, Qatar Airways, and Lufthansa, providing a healthy selection of operators to and from the Thai capital. The airport's position as a major international gateway to one of the world's most popular tourist destinations helps sustain its appeal among airlines operating busy long-haul routes.

Emirates, Qatar Airways and Etihad Airways provide important connections through their respective Gulf hubs, while Lufthansa and Asiana Airlines further diversify the airport's offering. Although Bangkok cannot match Dubai's flight frequency, its combination of aircraft numbers and airline variety makes it an attractive alternative. The presence of five operators also gives aviation enthusiasts a broader selection of aircraft to look out for on the Simple Flying Flight Tracker.

The US is a key long-haul market for superjumbo operators.

Sydney Kingsford Smith Airport (SYD) recorded 628 flights operated by the Airbus A380, placing it among the busiest airports in the world for the aircraft type. Airlines that operate the A380 to and from Sydney include Asiana Airlines, Singapore Airlines, Qantas, and Emirates, reflecting Australia's strong international travel market and demand for high-capacity long-haul services. The airport's extensive international connections make it an important destination for several of the world's remaining Superjumbo operators. Qantas is naturally an important part of the Sydney spotting experience, given its Australian headquarters and long-standing investment in the Airbus A380. Singapore Airlines and Emirates also contribute to the airport's activity, providing opportunities to see several different aircraft and liveries. This combination makes Sydney particularly interesting for those looking to compare the different configurations and appearances of international A380 operators.

Singapore Changi Airport (SIN) follows with a total of 895 flights operated by the Airbus A380 recorded between October 5 and November 3, 2026. British Airways, Qantas, Emirates, and Singapore Airlines all deploy their Superjumbos to the airport, reflecting its importance as a major destination for tourism, business, and transit flights. Singapore Airlines is particularly significant as the launch operator of the Airbus A380 back in 2007, and the aircraft remains part of its long-haul operations today, almost 20 years later.

London Heathrow Airport (LHR) saw 1,162 Airbus A380 flights, placing it second overall. However, its most impressive statistic concerns the number of different aircraft and airlines represented. Six different airlines operate the aircraft type to and from the UK's largest airport, which is also one of the busiest in the world for international traffic.

Airbus A380s from British Airways, Qantas, Emirates, Qatar Airways, Singapore Airlines, and Etihad Airways all appeared at the airport between October 5 and November 3, 2026. This makes London Heathrow particularly appealing for those looking to track a wide variety of different liveries.

With a total of 12 Airbus A380s in its fleet, British Airways is the joint-second largest operator of the aircraft, together with Singapore Airlines. The UK flag carrier deploys its A380s on selected long-haul services, including to Miami International Airport (MIA), San Francisco International Airport (SFO), and Singapore Changi Airport (SIN).

Emirates also maintains a significant presence with multiple daily frequencies, while Qatar Airways and Singapore Airlines connect London Heathrow with their respective international networks. Qantas and Etihad Airways provide further variety, ensuring that the airport regularly welcomes aircraft from several major global operators.

A closer look at how to fly on the A380 to and from Australia this year.

Dubai International Airport (DXB) is the undisputed center of the world's Airbus A380 operation, recording a staggering 4,285 flights during the same period. That figure is substantially higher than any other airport, reflecting the facility's importance as the primary hub for Emirates and making it the busiest Superjumbo spotting destination by a wide margin. Unlike airports where several airlines contribute to the Airbus A380 schedule, Dubai's operation is overwhelmingly dominated by Emirates. The airline has built much of its international network around the aircraft, deploying it on routes connecting the UAE with destinations across Europe, Asia, Africa, North America, and Australia. Its extensive fleet allows it to maintain an extraordinary number of daily services.

The latest data from ch-aviation shows that Emirates is by far the world's largest operator of the Airbus A380:

Dubai's position as a major connecting hub helps explain its extraordinary activity. Emirates uses the Airbus A380 to bring passengers together from across its network, with the aircraft providing substantial capacity on particularly busy routes. This creates repeated opportunities to observe different aircraft arriving and departing, with the airport's extensive international schedule keeping the Superjumbo firmly at the center of its operations.

Energy Transfer Just Raised Its Distribution for the 20th Straight Quarter. Here's How Much $10,000 Invested Pays Annually.

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Don't let a past dividend cut scare you away from this 7%-yielding pipeline stock.

Energy Transfer (ET -1.41%), one of the largest pipeline-focused master limited partnerships (MLPs), recently announced its 20th consecutive increase in its quarterly cash distribution. Raising its quarterly distribution by more than 3%, the company now pays $0.3425 per unit quarterly, or $1.37 per unit annually.

That said, given how Energy Transfer has implemented a dividend cut in the recent past, management's touting of its "20th consecutive quarterly increase" is a bit misleading. Yet, while Energy Transfer may lack the dividend growth track record of other pipeline stocks, that doesn't mean you need to stick this name in your "stay away" pile.

Based on Energy Transfer's current stock price of around $19 per share, a $10,000 investment would buy you around 526 units. Multiply that by the annualized distributions, and the total amount of distributions from this investment is around $720 per year.

This equates to around a 7.1% forward distribution yield, well above the S&P 500 current forward dividend yield of around 1.1%. This also represents a higher forward yield than peers, such as 6.1%-yielding Enterprise Products Partners and 5.7%-yielding Enbridge.

Energy Transfer may have cut distributions in the past, but I wouldn't anticipate this happening again anytime soon. For one, Energy Transfer's distribution cut was due primarily to the COVID-19 outbreak, a black swan event. Moreover, consider Energy Transfer's latest quarterly results. For the quarter ending Sept. 30, the MLP reported distributable cash flow of $2.5 billion versus $1.18 billion in distributions.

Energy Transfer is also pursuing acquisitions it believes are accretive to its distributable cash flow, such as its recently announced $2.6 billion deal for Vaquero Midstream, and continues to invest billions organically to expand its pipeline network. Although future performance hinges heavily on the macro backdrop for energy, until signs of an unfavorable shift emerge, consider Energy Transfer another top choice among pipeline stocks for income-focused investors.

Thomas Niel has no position in any of the stocks mentioned. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Enbridge. The Motley Fool recommends Enterprise Products Partners. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

Taiwanese telco Chunghwa Telecom announced a slate of film and series investments at Taiwan Creative Content Fest (TCCF), including police crime drama Gunshot, starring Kent Tsai, and Call Of Lobster, starring Choi Siwon and Chang Chen.

Chunghwa is one of three Taiwanese telcos that are investing in film and TV content through funds launched in partnership with the Taiwanese government.

The company’s previous content investments include feature films Arrested Memory, a Japan-Taiwan co-production directed by Sabu, and The Photo From 1977, directed by Frank Cheng and Sheng-Jung Tang. Other drama series investments from Chunghwa include Yesterday’s Daughter, Tomorrow’s Mother, a family fantasy series created by award-winning producer Phoebe Ma and director Hung Tzu-peng; fantasy adventure series Guides To The World Below, adapted from a popular Taiwanese comic; and C’est La Vie, an urban drama blending cuisine and women’s personal growth, with actress Esther Liu serving as executive producer.

Outside of films and series, Chunghwa is also backing reality cooking show Next Z Chef and indie band Shallow Levée’s Speak in Silence Asian tour.

At the slate announcement event in TCCF, Taiwan’s Minister of Culture Li Yuan said: “Chunghwa Telecom is like a pyramid. I am grateful that, beyond building a strong foundation through its thriving core business, the company is also willing to invest at the very top of the pyramid, supporting film and television professionals as they continue moving forward and creating even better work.”

Chunghwa Telecom Senior Executive Vice President Lin Wen-chiyh said: “Chunghwa Telecom is more than just a platform operator; we are also a driving force behind the long-term development of the content industry. Moving forward, we will continue to connect funding, creative talent, and domestic and international partners, leveraging our group’s resources and industry partnerships to help Taiwanese content reach international markets.”

Why you shouldn't remove the screen protector film on your Nintendo Switch 2

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

We're now a little over one year into the launch of the Nintendo Switch 2, which first hit the market back in the middle of 2025. It was priced in the same ballpark as its competitors, the PlayStation 5 and Xbox Series X/S, at $450 for the base console and $500 for a Mario Kart World bundle. Since then, it has increased to a standard $500, even for a base edition.

No one likes high prices, but if you paid either of those dollar amounts for the Switch 2, you've become keenly aware of your investment in this little machine. Keeping that investment safe is paramount, and one way to prevent damage is to avoid something that Nintendo has continuously warned us about even before launch: removing the pre-installed screen protector.

It may not seem like much, but it is a critical piece of the system's configuration. Removing it may seem tempting, but it's actually going to do quite a bit to protect your $500 investment.

When we buy a new piece of hardware or device, the satisfaction of peeling a film off is remarkably enjoyable. If you have a brand-new Switch 2, however, it's recommended that you absolutely not do that. It may seem like it's there for shipping needs, but its true purpose is rather important to the overall health and longevity of the system.

Calling it a "screen protector" isn't even entirely accurate, as it's described by Nintendo as an anti-scattering adhesive film. The main purpose of the film is to protect the console from glass shards that may shatter if the screen is broken. Needless to say, it plays a critical role in the system's lifespan.

Thankfully, actually removing it is quite difficult. Removing the film requires some intense force, which is probably the first and only clue one needs to determine that it shouldn't be taken off. If you do, somehow, manage to get it off, the system will be in severe danger. It is a foundational part of the console's hardware.

Now that you know why you shouldn't ever remove the adhesive film from the console, you may be curious to know if the Switch 2 needs any extra protection. While you will likely get by just fine with Nintendo's pre-installed protection, there are some extra steps you can take to give your Switch 2 a bit of extra life in the years ahead.

Nintendo sells an affordable screen protector that is manufactured by HORI, and there are so many third-party aftermarket versions available for purchase that it would be almost impossible for you to not be able to find one you like. Unlike the one applied by Nintendo, an actual screen protector will do exactly that, safeguarding the console from scratches and cosmetic damage.

Tempered glass protectors will cost slightly more, depending on the brand, but this is a part of the console's accessory lineup that is actually reasonably priced when compared to other items. If you're at all worried about the screen on your Switch 2, your best course of action is to do as instructed by Nintendo and leave the film on, then grab yourself a decently priced tempered glass screen protector.

Appalachian Trail Gear: What Worked, What Didn’t, and What I Changed (Part 1)

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

I found that, just like in the real world, things break, wear out, and sometimes need to be replaced. Just as you budget for an unexpected car repair, you need to plan for gear replacements on the trail. Your shoes aren’t going to last the entire hike (mine lasted about 500 miles!), and sometimes things break when you least expect them.

Take my sleeping pad, for example. It unexpectedly started losing air, and thankfully, my hiking buddy let me borrow his “trail couch,” a foam sleeping pad, until I made it to Daleville, Virginia. Then there are the upgrades you might choose to make along the way. Maybe you realize you can get by with a smaller, lighter tent and decide it’s worth the investment to shed some weight from your pack.

The point is, preparing for a thru-hike isn’t just about buying all your gear before you start. It’s also about budgeting for the things that will wear out, break, or simply need an upgrade along the way. Just like maintaining a car or keeping up with home repairs, taking care of your gear is part of the journey.

I started and ended with a Base Weight of about 13.5 lbs without food, fuel, or water.

So, let’s look at the gear I started with, what held up, what I replaced along the way, and what I carried to the finish line.

I won’t go into a deep dive on my entire gear list, but if you’re curious about the specifics, you can check out my full gear list that I started with on my profile here and what I finished with on LighterPack here.

What I Started With: Big Agnes Tiger Wall UL2

What I Finished With: Hyperlite Mountain Gear Crosspeak 1

I had plenty of experience with the gear I started with, and honestly, it worked! I loved pretty much everything I owned, so I didn’t see a reason to replace perfectly good gear just to upgrade to the latest and trendiest options. That said, I did budget for a new tent.

Originally, I planned to start with the Zpacks Duplex, a tent my fiancé and I use on our backpacking trips. The only catch? He’s usually the one who sets it up for us. Since this was going to be a solo adventure, I needed to be confident setting up my own shelter. It was the middle of winter in Vermont before my hike, and after a few unsuccessful attempts at practicing on my own in the snow, I just wasn’t feeling confident enough to bring it along.

Instead, I decided to start with my Big Agnes Tiger Wall UL2. At 2.5 pounds, it’s a freestanding tent that’s practically dummy-proof, offers plenty of space, and, most importantly, was something I had tons of experience setting up on my own.

First time pitching my new Crosspeak 1 tent.

I love freestanding tents, but I had my eye on a Dyneema version. The one I wanted wasn’t scheduled to hit the market until April, so my plan was to use the Tiger Wall until it launched. Easy enough, right? Well, then a material issue delayed the release.

Meanwhile, Hyperlite Mountain Gear launched its CrossPeak 1 at Trail Days, and they were offering a deal on it. I didn’t hesitate for a second. I may have downsized on space, but this tent checked a lot of boxes for me and was worth the upgrade.

What I Started With: Gossamer Gear Gorilla 50L

What I Finished With: Gossamer Gear Mariposa 60L

I’ve been a huge fan of Gossamer Gear packs ever since hiking the Wonderland Trail in 2023. In 2024, I invested in the Gorilla 50L for my Tahoe Rim Trail thru-hike, and it turned out to be the perfect pack for me and my needs. So naturally, I figured I’d take it along for the Appalachian Trail. No regrets… at least at first!

The first two months on the AT were pretty dry, and I sometimes found myself carrying up to 4L of water to ‘dry camps’. Resupply options were also more spread out than I expected, which meant carrying five to six days of food at a time. The Gorilla is designed to comfortably carry around 25 pounds, with a recommended maximum of 30 pounds, and I found myself pushing those limits pretty regularly.

My beloved Gorilla started ripping on both sides of the bottom corners.

Eventually, my lower back started chafing from the pack rubbing against it, and the side seams began to split. I was so sad because I genuinely loved this pack! The removable back foam pad doubles as both a frame and a sit pad, which is such a great feature. The side pockets are positioned at just the right height, making it SUPER easy to grab my water bottle and slide it back in without stopping or asking someone for help. And the large exterior mesh pocket? Another favorite.

Rather than give up on Gossamer Gear altogether, I decided to stick with the brand and upgrade to its larger pack, the Mariposa 60L. With a maximum recommended load of around 35 pounds, it seemed like the perfect solution for my heavier carries. I never ended up maxing it out, and it gave me the extra capacity I needed.

Eventually, I also got better at dialing in my resupplies, and my water carries dropped back down to around 2L. But even with the lighter loads, I struggled with the Mariposa for a long time. I’m not entirely sure why, but it felt and sat so differently from my Gorilla. I even had the Gossamer Gear team fit me at Trail Days, yet I still felt like the pack I’d purchased was too small for me.

Over time, it seemed to get more comfortable. Or maybe I just got used to the pain and discomfort. Honestly, I’m still torn about whether the Mariposa will be the pack I reach for on my next thru-hike or if I’ll be looking elsewhere. I loved my Gorilla, but my experience with the Mariposa left me with some mixed feelings about what pack comes next.

Navigating the large boulders on the way up Mama K, featuring my Mariposa.

What I Started With: Enlightened Equipment Enigma 20-degree down

What I Finished With: Enlightened Equipment Revelation APEX 40-degree synthetic

I started the trail March 9th with a 20-degree down quilt, but by the time I reached Pearisburg, Virginia, I was ready to make the switch to my 40-degree synthetic quilt.

For me, the 20-degree quilt just wasn’t the right fit for the entire trail. I tend to run hot, and even early on, I found myself getting too warm at night. I was more than happy to swap it out for the lighter, cooler 40-degree option when I had the chance.

Of course, there were still colder nights ahead, especially once I reached the White Mountains in New Hampshire. When temperatures dropped, I made do with what I had, occasionally pulling my rain pants over my wool baselayer tights for a little extra warmth.

Looking back, I’m glad I made the switch. I didn’t need a 20-degree quilt for the entire trail, and having the option to adjust my sleep system as the seasons and temperatures changed worked out really well for me.

What I used: Therm-a-rest NeoAir Xlite Regular Wide

Good sleep is SO important on the trail. When you’re hiking all day, having a comfortable place to rest and recover at night makes a huge difference. I’ve had this sleeping pad for a few years now, and I absolutely love it.

I originally had the regular size but found it too narrow, especially as a woman and a side sleeper. So, I invested in the Regular Wide, and it’s been perfect for me. This version also has an improved inflation valve, and I find it a bit quieter than the original model and some of the other sleeping pads on the market.

Remember what I mentioned earlier about budgeting for gear repairs and replacements? Well, here’s a perfect example.

5-mile trail NERO, it dumped rain, and many of us called it a day and squeezed into a shelter by 2 pm.

I’m guessing that on my last night before reaching Pearisburg, Virginia, I managed to puncture my sleeping pad. It was pouring rain, and honestly, I was just happy to find a spot, get my tent up, and crawl into something dry. Somewhere in that process, I must have pitched my tent over a stick or something sharp.

I didn’t notice anything was wrong until my first night back on the trail after taking a zero in Pearisburg. My pad was noticeably deflated. I figured it was probably just the temperature change, so I blew it back up and went to sleep. Except I found myself doing that two more times before morning.

I tried all the usual tricks to find the leak. I inflated the pad, pushed down on it, listened for escaping air, and ran my hands all over the surface, hoping to feel even the tiniest bit of air escaping. I even attempted the water-and-soap trick, but no luck. I just couldn’t find the hole.

Thankfully, my trail buddy Viking came to the rescue and lent me his foam sleeping pad until we reached Daleville, Virginia. I was incredibly grateful, but I’m not going to lie, those few nights sleeping on what felt like the bare ground were rough!

When it came time to replace it, there wasn’t much debating what to buy. I loved this sleeping pad enough to purchase the exact same one. Sometimes you find gear that just works for you, and there’s no reason to mess with a good thing.

These trekking poles were a gift from my fiancé, and one of the things I loved most about them was how incredibly lightweight they are. They’re among the lightest options out there, weighing 4.9oz per pole. I was a little surprised that I didn’t see more of them on the trail. Maybe it’s the nearly $200 price tag, or perhaps people are hesitant about investing in carbon fiber poles because of the possibility of them breaking.

I saw plenty of bent poles and broken tips along the way, but guess what? My LT5s survived all 2,200 miles without a single bent shaft or even needing a replacement tip!

Well, almost all 2,200 miles. One of my poles did decide to go on its own solo adventure by leaping into the Delaware River. I had set the pair down while stopping to take a photo at the Pennsylvania–New Jersey state line, and apparently, one decided it had somewhere else to be. I’ll give Gossamer Gear this: these poles do float! Unfortunately, I couldn’t get the attention of any of the paddlers below to help rescue it.

The one pole that decided to stay with me to Katahdin. The other went on its own solo adventure into the Delaware.

I reached out to Gossamer Gear, and they were incredibly kind and sent me a replacement. So, while I technically lost one pole to the Delaware, I was able to finish with a full set.

My one complaint with these poles is the twist-lock adjustment mechanism. It tends to get stuck, and there were plenty of times I had to wrestle with it to get the poles adjusted. Sometimes I’d even hand them over to Jerzy to manhandle them for me!

Despite that one frustration, I was really happy with these poles. They’re lightweight, held up remarkably well over the entire trail, and I’d happily recommend them to someone looking to save weight on their setup.

What I Finished With: Altra Olympus 275 Matryx

I’ve been an Altra fan since 2018. I’ve tried Hoka, I’ve tried Topo, and nothing has quite compared to the zero-drop platform and roomy toe box that Altra offers.

I started the AT with the Olympus 6. I’ll admit, I wasn’t completely sold on this version when I first tried it the summer before my thru-hike. The fit felt a little different from previous versions, and I wasn’t a fan of that odd little heel pillow. But overall, the shoe worked well for me, and I got about 500 miles out of that first pair.

New on the right vs. old pair on the left that I had since Marion, VA, to 501 Shelter in PA.

One of my favorite things about the Olympus is the extra cushioning. When you’re putting in miles day after day, that added cushion makes a big difference. I did experience some heel blisters, particularly along the sides of my heels. Over time, those eventually turned into pretty impressive calluses. As the shoes approached the end of their life, the blisters and foot fatigue became my signal that it was time for a new pair.

That decision came when I stopped at Marion Outdoors to replace my shoes. That’s where I was introduced to the Olympus 275 Matryx. It offered the familiar fit and features I loved, but instead of the heel pillow, it had a built-in gaiter guard.

I still preferred wearing my separate gaiters with these shoes. Although the built-in gaiter guard meant there weren’t traditional gaiter attachment points, I found a workaround. I simply hooked the front of my gaiters onto my shoelaces. The outfitter initially gave me some adhesive Velcro to help secure them, but that eventually peeled off. Surprisingly, the gaiters still stayed pretty well in place without it.

Altra Olympus 275, featuring how I’d attache my gaiters to the shoe laces.

All in all, I went through five pairs of shoes during my thru-hike. I probably could have squeezed the entire trail into four pairs, but I wanted my final 120 miles to be as comfortable and pain-free as possible. My general rule of thumb became replacing my shoes around every 500 miles. If I tried to push them much further, I’d quickly pay for it with blisters and foot fatigue.

After 2,200 miles, I was pretty happy with that system. Your shoes are one piece of gear I’d absolutely budget for before starting a thru-hike. They’re not going to last forever, and waiting until your feet are telling you it’s time for a new pair isn’t always the best strategy!

That covers the gear that carried me through the miles, but what about everything I wore along the way? From socks and underwear to the layers that got me through everything from hot Virginia afternoons to chilly New Hampshire nights, I’ll be breaking down my clothing system in the next post.

BWX Technologies: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Plant expansions and new defense manufacturing capacity position the company to convert a surging contract pipeline into sustained multiyear revenue growth.

On its third-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Nov. 2, 2026, BWX Technologies (BWXT +3.97%) reported 10% top-line growth to $953 million and raised the low end of its full-year free cash flow guidance. The nuclear component manufacturer continues to capitalize on rising global energy security needs, converting sovereign and commercial interest into multiyear defense contracts. Three key insights from management stand out for long-term investors.

The company's Government Operations segment generated a 22.6% adjusted EBITDA margin (earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation, and amortization, which is a measure of core operational cash flow) as high-purity depleted uranium (HPDU, a dense material used in strategic defense applications) programs scaled up. BWX Technologies started constructing a dedicated manufacturing plant to support long-term defense demand.

The Commercial Operations segment posted 25% total revenue growth while expanding physical operations to handle surging nuclear power market demand. The company finished enlarging its Canadian facility to execute on backlog for reactor refurbishments and small modular reactors (SMRs, scaled-down nuclear power plants built in factories).

Commercial and military demand for specialized microreactors expanded across global markets. The company expanded its contract pipeline for the BWXT Advanced Nuclear Reactor and tri-structural isotropic (TRISO, a high-temperature resistant nuclear fuel) manufacturing lines.

Nuclear demand is accelerating across national security and commercial power markets. BWX Technologies leverages high-barrier manufacturing assets and proprietary fuel processing to maintain operational momentum.

Capital allocation prioritizes organic facility expansions while management tracks commercial bid pipeline conversion into firm multiyear contracts.

Keanu Reeves’ Iconic ’90s Crime Thriller Is Officially Being Removed From Netflix

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Keanu Reeves is one of the biggest action stars in the world, but there have been times in his career where it looked like he was on the out. One of those times came around 2013, when Reeves starred in one of the biggest box office and critical bombs of his career, 47 Ronin.

It wasn’t until the release of John Wick one year later that his career was back on track, and he has since starred in several John Wick projects and other successful movies, like Toy Story 4. Reeves is one of the few stars who has been around long enough that he’s now widely known and loved for two different roles, John Wick from the action franchise of the same name, and Neo from The Matrix movies, who he’s played for over 20 years.

If there was ever a time before now when Keanu Reeves’ stardom wasn’t in question, it was the late '90s and early 2000s. In 1999, Reeves made his debut as Neo in The Matrix, and he has since reprised that role in three sequels releasing as recently as 2021. However, a few years before the first Matrix movie, Reeves starred in one of the all-time great action films, Speed, alongside Dennis Hopper and Sandra Bullock in a career-launching role.

But three years before Speed hit theaters, Reeves could be seen opposite Gary Busey, Lori Petty, and the late Patrick Swayze in the classic heist thriller, Point Break, which grossed $84 million at the box office against a $24 million budget. The film has found a home streaming on Netflix in the last few months, but the streamer recently announced that the last day to watch the movie on the platform will be on November 30.

Point Break was one of the first movies from acclaimed director Kathryn Bigelow, who returned to the screen last year with her work on A House of Dynamite, the Netflix war thriller starring Rebecca Ferguson and Idris Elba. Bigelow is also known for her work on Zero Dark Thirty and The Hurt Locker, which won her two Oscars: Best Director and Best Picture.

The official synopsis for Point Break, which earned scores of 69% from critics and 79% from audiences on Rotten Tomatoes, reads as follows:

“After a string of bizarre bank robberies in Southern California, with the crooks donning masks of various former presidents, a federal agent, Johnny Utah (Keanu Reeves), infiltrates the suspected gang. But this is no ordinary group of robbers. They're surfers — led by the charismatic Bodhi (Patrick Swayze) — who are addicted to the rush of thievery. But when Utah falls in love with a female surfer, Tyler (Lori Petty), who is close to the gang, it complicates his sense of duty.”

Check out Point Break before it is removed from Netflix later this month and stay tuned to Collider for more updates and coverage of Keanu Reeves’ future projects.

Tom Cruise’s High-Flying $135M Thriller Officially Lands on a New Streamer

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Tom Cruise has spent decades risking life and limb for the sake of our entertainment, whether he's hanging off airplanes, jumping motorcycles off cliffs, or pushing fighter jets to their absolute limits. But while Top Gun: Maverick and the Mission: Impossible franchise have turned his death-defying stunts into a selling point all of their own, one of his most entertaining thrillers takes a slightly different approach to aviation. Instead of saving the world, he's making an absolute fortune breaking the law. And somehow, it's all based on a true story.

Cruise's 2017 crime thriller American Made has officially landed on Prime Video in the United States, joining the streaming service's November lineup on November 1. Directed by Doug Liman, the movie earned approximately $135.6 million worldwide and reunites Cruise with the filmmaker behind Edge of Tomorrow.

One of the wealthiest men in 1980s America is someone you’ve never heard of. With his devilish swagger and zest for life, TWA pilot Barry Seal (Cruise) is the hero of his sleepy Southern town. Alabama Power spent more than $6 million on ads last year — and customers can be billed for half of them until 2029

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

This article originally appeared on Inside Climate News, a nonprofit, non-partisan news organization that covers climate, energy and the environment. Sign up for their newsletter here.

On a busy election day, the Alabama Public Service Commission requested that Alabama Power voluntarily stop billing customers, effective immediately, for television commercials and other ads it buys to promote various aspects of its business.

Alabama Power is the largest electricity provider in Alabama, with territory covering about two-thirds of the state. Though it is a monopoly, the company spends millions each year on advertising, touting its reliability, customer service and other aspects of its business.

In recent months, the company has launched a series of television commercials touting a rate freeze it announced late last year amid heavy criticism over rising electricity prices.

A state law passed earlier this year will prohibit Alabama Power from including the costs of those commercials in customers’ electric bills, meaning the powerful utility will have to find all of the dollars in corporate profits. But the new law won’t take effect until 2029.

Currently, Alabama Power is allowed to bill customers for half of its non-public safety advertising costs.

During Tuesday’s meeting, PSC President Cynthia Lee Almond moved to “request” that Alabama Power voluntarily begin excluding those costs immediately.

“I don’t see any reason why we shouldn’t go ahead and take steps now to begin that process, rather than waiting until Jan. 1, 2029,” Almond said during the meeting.

Almond then made her motion, which was not on the meeting agenda, but came after the regular report from the commission’s electricity division.

“I move that the commission request that Alabama Power consent to exclude all advertising expenses from retail rate setting until after Dec. 31, 2028, when the new law takes effect,” Almond said. “Assuming Alabama Power consents, this interim measure would be reflected in its [Rate Stabilization and Equalization] filings for 2027 and 2028.”

Commissioners Jeremy Oden and Chris Beeker IV voted yes after a brief discussion.

According to the company filings with the Federal Energy Regulatory Commission, Alabama Power spent more than $6 million in general advertising last year, roughly $4 per customer. It’s unclear from the federal filing how much of that total was passed on to customers or how much might be impacted by the new prohibition.

Alabama Power did not immediately respond to a request for comment.

Daniel Tait, executive director of Energy Alabama, said the move was a boon to Alabama Power customers, who faced some of the highest electric bills in the country in recent years.

“We are grateful for the leadership of President Almond and the full commission for acting to end customers paying for Alabama Power’s advertising,” Tait said. “Once in place, Alabama will have one of the strongest customer protections in the nation on this front.”

This will be among the last actions of the commission under its current construction before a major overhaul takes effect.

In January, the commission will expand from three members to seven. Outgoing Gov. Kay Ivey has appointed four new commissioners, who will take office in January, including the PSC’s two first-ever Black members.

Oden and Beeker will also leave the commission in January after being defeated in Republican primaries. Cullman County Sheriff Matt Gentry will take Oden’s Place 1 seat, and former state auditor and PSC Commissioner Jim Zeigler will replace Beeker in Place 2.

Zeigler, a perennial candidate in Alabama across several decades, will return to the commission 48 years after leaving the PSC. He served one term from 1975-1979, during an era of skyrocketing energy costs and protracted political battles over electric rates between Alabama Power and then-Gov. George Wallace.

Almond will be the sole remaining member of the current commission.

The new seven-member commission will be overseen by the state’s first secretary of energy, who will be selected by Gov.-elect Tommy Tuberville. The new secretary will set the commission agenda, and overruling the secretary will require a vote of five of seven commissioners.

Tuberville has not yet announced his pick for secretary of energy. The only statutory requirement is that the secretary “must have experience in utility regulation or energy policy or a related field.”

Novo Nordisk Just Delivered Great News for Investors. Is a Comeback Finally Underway?

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Novo got a much-needed boost from its latest earnings report.

Are things finally starting to look up for Novo (NVO +0.03%)? The Denmark-based pharmaceutical leader has had a rough past couple of years, but its third-quarter financial results were strong enough to send the stock up 6% post-earnings. That's great news for shareholders, but can Novo maintain this momentum?

At first glance, Novo's third-quarter results didn’t seem particularly impressive, as some of the company’s longtime growth drivers underperformed. For instance, sales of Ozempic, the company's famous diabetes medicine, were 29.7 billion Danish kroner (about $4.5 billion), down 3% compared to the year-ago period. The company's weight-loss medicine, Wegovy, recorded sales of 20.4 billion DKK (about $3.1 billion), roughly flat compared with the prior-year quarter.

However, several other medicines helped pick up the slack, and arguably none was more important than Novo's oral Wegovy. The company launched this weight loss pill -- the first of its kind to earn approval -- in January. The Wegovy pill has helped attract many patients who were hesitant to start treatment with injectables. Novo reported that, as of June, oral Wegovy had surpassed three million prescriptions, and more than 80% were for patients who had never taken GLP-1s before. In the third quarter, oral Wegovy generated 5.4 billion DKK (about $810 million) in revenue.

Novo's total net sales were 79.2 billion DKK (about $11.9 billion), up 6% year over year. The company's net income dropped by 16% year over year to 16.7 billion DKK (about $2.5 billion), largely due to non-cash impairment charges. Still, Novo’s results beat analyst estimates, and the pharmaceutical company also raised its guidance for the fiscal year 2026.

It now expects revenue to be flat (at the midpoint) year over year, while the previous guidance implied a 3% year-over-year drop at the midpoint. It's no wonder Novo's shares jumped following this beat-and-raise quarter. But what's next for the drugmaker?

There are several catalysts ahead that could help Novo improve its financial performance. Let's consider three of them. First, the company could make meaningful progress with oral Wegovy. The medicine has now been approved in several other countries, including Germany and the U.K.

These markets might not be as lucrative as the U.S., but they should move the needle for the company. Novo will also likely attract brand-new GLP-1 patients in these regions. The company is also looking to earn label expansions for oral Wegovy. Novo has submitted an application to the U.S. Food and Drug Administration for oral Wegovy as a potential treatment for metabolic dysfunction-associated steatohepatitis.

The original injectable version of Wegovy is approved for this indication, but the pill offers a more convenient option. It could be successful in this niche and continue driving Novo's sales higher. Second, Novo will launch new products to strengthen its position in the diabetes and weight-loss markets.

It is inching closer to approval for CagriSema, a medicine that beat Wegovy in a head-to-head Phase 3 clinical trial for weight loss. CagriSema also posted strong clinical trial results in diabetes. This medicine could help Novo steal some market share from its biggest competitor, Eli Lilly (LLY -0.79%).

Third, Novo has several pipeline candidates that could improve its approved lineup over time. The company's Phase 3 pipeline features zenagamtide, a promising diabetes and weight-loss medicine that is being developed in subcutaneous and oral formulations.

The weight loss market is growing rapidly, and this product, along with others, may help Novo ride that tailwind for a long time, even as competition intensifies. Novo may still experience some clinical setbacks, but after the beating it took over the past two years, the company is slowly staging a comeback. In my view, the stock is attractive following its third-quarter results.

Prosper Junior Bakiny has positions in Eli Lilly and Novo Nordisk. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Eli Lilly and Novo Nordisk. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.
